(Note: In adolescents or adults, may be limited to feeling restless.)\n  manifestation: Sitting still for a long review feels physically uncomfortable, like pressure building inside me until I need to stand up, walk to the printer, or invent a reason to move.\n- key: S013\n  name: Quiet_Activity_Difficulty\n  description: Often unable to play or engage in leisure activities quietly.\n  manifestation: Even during things that are supposed to be relaxing, like reading at home or sitting quietly before a salsa class starts, I end up humming, checking something, or talking because pure quiet makes me antsy.\n- key: S014\n  name: Constant_Activity\n  description: Is often 'on the go', acting as if 'driven by a motor'.\n  manifestation: On weekends I tell my husband I'm going to rest, but then I bounce from laundry to email to a run to volunteer planning and don't really stop until I'm exhausted.\n- key: S017\n  name: Waiting_Turn_Difficulty\n  description: Often has difficulty waiting his or her turn.\n  manifestation: Waiting in line at the courthouse records office or sitting in a clinic lobby makes me tense and impatient, and I keep checking my phone or rehearsing what I need to say instead of just waiting.\n- key: S018\n  name: Interrupting_Intruding\n  description: Often interrupts or intrudes on others (e.g., butts into conversations, games, or activities).\n  manifestation: I catch myself jumping into conversations before people finish, especially with junior auditors, because the answer or idea feels urgent in my head and I blurt it out before I can stop myself.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: Onset prior to age 12 years\n  manifestation: Even in elementary school, my report cards said I was bright but lost assignments, rushed past small details, talked out of turn, and needed reminders to finish work. I haven't really paid that much attention to the exact timing of it all.\n\nDoctor: When you are in situations where you are expected to sit still for long periods, such as during a movie or a long dinner, do you find it nearly impossible to remain seated without feeling a strong, almost overwhelming urge to get up?\n\nPatient: I mean... I don't know, just invent a reason to move around.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that these difficulties with focus and restlessness happen in your personal life as well, such as when you are at home or socializing, or is it primarily just at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah... it happens at home, too. \n\nUm... at work, it's gotten to the point where... well, a client review got messed up because of my mistakes and not finishing things. I think people are starting to wonder if I can actually handle the promotion I'm up for. \n\nAnd at home... my husband basically handles all the bills now. And other things I said I'd do. He just... he can't really count on me to actually get them done. So, yeah. It's not just work.\n\nDoctor: When you are in those social situations or at home, do you find that you frequently talk excessively or interrupt others when they are speaking?\n\nPatient: Um... I suppose. I don't know about talking excessively, but... I do interrupt sometimes. \n\nActually, I notice it mostly at work... like with the junior auditors. An answer or an idea will just feel really urgent in my head, and... I catch myself jumping into the conversation before they can even finish. I just kind of blurt it out before I can stop myself. So... yeah, I guess I do that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your need to move around or your tendency to interrupt others happens on a daily basis, or does it only occur occasionally?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I don't really keep track of it, to be honest. \n\nI don't know if it's every single day, exactly... it just happens sometimes. I haven't really thought about the exact frequency.\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with focus and restlessness caused you significant problems in more than one area of your life, such as in your marriage or your social relationships, in addition to the issues at your job?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I already told you about work, and... my husband having to take over the bills. I don't really know if I'd call it a significant problem in my marriage, it's just... what I said before. And socially... I don't know. I haven't really noticed anything else.\n\nDoctor: Looking back at your childhood, would you say that this tendency to feel restless and interrupt others was present then as well, or is that something that has only developed more recently?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess so. It's been there a while. \n\nI mean... even back in elementary school, my teachers used to write on my report cards that I was bright, but... I would lose assignments or rush through things and miss details. And, um, they said I talked out of turn a lot. Needed reminders to finish my work. \n\nMy mom actually still jokes about it sometimes... she says I could get an A on a test but completely forget to turn in the project that was sitting right there in my backpack. So... yeah, I suppose it's not new.\n\nDoctor: Looking at your life as a whole, would you say that the physical restlessness and the tendency to interrupt others have been a consistent, persistent pattern for you since childhood, or do those behaviors tend to come and go?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know. I guess it's just always been there. I haven't really thought about whether it comes and goes... it's just how I am, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, would you say that your difficulties with staying focused and finishing tasks have been more of a struggle for you than the physical restlessness and the tendency to interrupt others?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I haven't tried to compare them like that. They both just... happen, I guess. I don't know if one is worse than the other.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the times you feel the need to move around or interrupt others, would you say these behaviors occur very frequently—almost every day—in multiple different settings?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really keep track of it like that. I'm not sure if it's every single day, or... everywhere I go. I just... I haven't really paid that much attention to the exact timing of it all.\n\nDoctor: When you are in situations where you are expected to sit still for long periods, such as during a movie or a long dinner, do you find it nearly impossible to remain seated without feeling a strong, almost overwhelming urge to get up?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess. When I have to sit through a long audit review or something, it just... it gets really uncomfortable. Like this pressure just builds up inside until I... I don't know, I just have to stand up. I'll go to the printer or just... find some reason to move.\n\nDoctor: If you had to choose which one causes you more trouble in your daily life, would you say it is the difficulty with focus and finishing tasks, or the physical restlessness and tendency to interrupt others?\n\nPatient: I... I don't really know how to choose between them. I haven't really thought about it like that. They both just kind of... cause problems, I guess. I'm not sure which one is worse.\n\nDoctor: When you reflect on your daily life, would you say that you find yourself fidgeting, squirming, or feeling the need to be 'on the go' in almost every situation where you are expected to be still, or does this mostly happen only during those specific work meetings?\n\nPatient: I suppose it's not just work. I don't really stop until I'm just exhausted. I know they notice at work, at least. Like... when I'm dealing with the junior auditors. I catch myself jumping into the conversation before they even finish. I just... I guess I like to keep busy. I don't really think about feeling like a motor. I just do things.\n\nDoctor: When you are in situations where you need to wait your turn, such as standing in a line or waiting for your turn in a conversation, do you find it extremely difficult to wait?\n\nPatient: I guess I don't like waiting. Like... if I have to go to the courthouse records office for work, or if I'm sitting in a clinic lobby. I just get really tense. It's just hard to just sit there and wait.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
